## Day 3  (situations: sick)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:00 Wake, wash, dress in work clothes, and eat a quiet breakfast in the kitchen. (kitchen); 07:30 Make tea for Julian, carry it to his room, check his temperature, and put painkillers and a water bottle within reach. (bedroom_2); 08:15 Make coffee, carry it to Julian's desk, and set the tea bag and brass spoon on the kitchen sink-cabinet countertop. (kitchen); 08:30 Edit the active manuscript at Julian's desk, checking the proof pages against the printed style sheet and recording notes. (bedroom_2); 12:30 Make a light salad and eat it at the dining table while listening to the radio. (kitchen); 13:15 Rinse the lunch dishes, make another coffee, and check whether Julian needs more water or tea. (kitchen); 13:30 Continue editing and take the scheduled client calls, keeping a concise list of follow-up points. (bedroom_2); 17:30 Save the current work, return the proof pages to the canvas folder, and write a short list for tomorrow. (bedroom_2); 17:45 Make a fresh cup of tea for Julian, check his temperature again, and sit nearby briefly to see whether he needs anything. (bedroom_2); 18:15 Wash my hands, change into comfortable clothes, and prepare a simple dinner of toast with melted cheddar for both of us. (kitchen); 19:15 Carry dinner and water to the living room, then clear the kitchen surfaces enough for the morning. (living_room); 19:30 Read or watch a quiet program with Julian, pausing to check on him and leaving the book face-down with its receipt on the small shelving unit by the sofa. (living_room); 21:30 Wash up, check Julian's temperature once more, put painkillers and water beside him, and make sure he is settled before leaving him to rest. (bedroom_2); 22:00 Wash my face and brush my teeth, change into sleep clothes, and put my reading glasses on the nightstand by my bed. (bedroom_1); 22:30 Put my phone on charge by the wardrobe, read a few pages in bed, and go to sleep. (bedroom_1)
> *mara thinks:* Julian's illness changes the evening and makes the morning more watchful than usual. I will keep my normal editing schedule where possible, check his temperature, make tea, and take over dinner rather than assuming he will cook. I will stay home instead of taking the usual walk so I can check on him without making the day feel dramatic.

(bedroom_2); 07:35 Send the museum a brief message explaining that I am ill and will not commute, then put on comfortable clothes. (bedroom_2); 16:15 Rest quietly, dozing when possible and asking for help only if I need it. > *julian thinks:* I woke feeling properly unwell rather than merely tired: probably a short-lived bug, with poor sleep and little appetite. I will message the museum early, cancel the commute, and keep the day deliberately simple. I can make tea and toast myself, but I will order dinner rather than cook. I should avoid hovering around Mara while she works, though I may need to ask for small bits of help if I get worse.

## Day 4  (situations: sick)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:00 Wake, wash, dress in work clothes, and eat a quiet breakfast in the kitchen. I check on Julian afterward without disturbing him for long. (bedroom_1); 07:30 Make ginger tea for Julian, carry it to his room, check his temperature, and put painkillers and a water bottle within reach. I return to the kitchen and place my tea bag and brass spoon on the sink-cabinet countertop. (kitchen); 08:00 Make coffee, carry it and my laptop to the second bedroom, and settle the printed style sheet and proof pages on the desk. I send Julian a brief message asking him to call if he needs anything. (bedroom_2); 08:30 Edit the active manuscript at Julian's desk, checking the proof pages against the printed style sheet and recording notes. I pause once to check whether Julian needs more tea or water. (bedroom_2); 12:30 Make a light lunch, carry it to the dining table, and eat while listening to the radio. I rinse the dishes afterward and leave the kitchen reasonably clear. (kitchen); 13:15 Make fresh tea for Julian, carry it to his room, check his temperature, and replace the water bottle if needed. (bedroom_2); 13:30 Continue editing and take the scheduled client calls. I keep a concise list of follow-up points and save the manuscript before ending the work block. (bedroom_2); 17:30 Save the current work, return the proof pages to the canvas folder, and write tomorrow's short task list. I check on Julian once more before going out. (bedroom_2); 18:00 Make a brief grocery run for vegetables and a few easy foods, carrying the canvas tote and keys. (out_of_house); 18:45 Put away the groceries, wash my hands, and prepare a simple dinner of toast with melted cheddar and sliced vegetables. I carry Julian's portion and water to him, then eat mine at the dining table. (kitchen); 19:30 Read or watch a quiet program with Julian, checking discreetly whether he needs water or another blanket. When I finish reading, I leave the book face-down with its receipt marking the page on the small shelving unit by the sofa. (living_room); 21:30 Wash up, check Julian's temperature once more, put painkillers and water beside him, and make sure he is settled before leaving him to rest. (bedroom_2); 22:00 Wash my face and brush my teeth, change into sleep clothes, and put my reading glasses on the nightstand by my bed. (bathroom_1); 22:30 Put my phone on charge by the wardrobe, read a few pages in bed, and go to sleep. (bedroom_1)
> *mara thinks:* Julian is still unwell on the second day, so I will keep my usual editing schedule but build in quiet checks, tea, water, and temperature checks. I will take over dinner rather than expect him to cook, and I will make the grocery run brief and practical. I am somewhat tired from being watchful, but the routine and a settled apartment keep my stress low.

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:00 Wake, wash my face, brush my teeth, dress in work clothes, and check quietly whether Julian needs anything without disturbing him for long. (bedroom_1); 07:20 Make coffee and a quiet breakfast, carrying the mug and food to the dining table. I leave tea bags and the brass spoon on the kitchen sink-cabinet countertop for later. (kitchen); 07:50 Prepare ginger tea and carry it with painkillers and a fresh water bottle to Julian, checking his temperature and settling the items within reach. (kitchen); 08:10 Return to the second bedroom, open the laptop, take the active manuscript from the canvas folder, and arrange the proof pages and printed style sheet on the desk. (bedroom_2); 08:30 Edit the manuscript against the printed style sheet, recording precise notes and saving the file as I go. (bedroom_2); 10:30 Stand, stretch, and check whether Julian needs more tea, water, or medication. I refill the mug or bottle if needed and return to the desk. (bedroom_2); 10:40 Continue editing, reread difficult passages aloud under my breath, and save the revised manuscript before closing the morning work block. (bedroom_2); 12:30 Make a light lunch in the kitchen, carry it to the dining table, and eat while listening to the radio. I rinse the dishes and leave the kitchen reasonably clear. (kitchen); 13:15 Return to the desk and finish the weekly invoices, checking amounts and drafting any brief follow-up messages for unpaid accounts. (bedroom_2); 14:45 Take a short break and check on Julian, bringing fresh ginger tea or water if needed and checking his temperature quietly. (bedroom_2); 15:00 Back up the week's files, clear the desk, return the proof pages to the canvas folder, and write a concise list for Monday. (bedroom_2); 16:30 Make an easy early dinner for both of us: toast with melted cheddar and sliced vegetables. I carry Julian's portion and water to him, then leave my plate covered in the kitchen for later. (kitchen); 17:15 Wash my hands, change into walking clothes, put on walking shoes, and check once more that Julian has water and can call me if needed. (bedroom_1); 17:45 Walk around the neighborhood at a brisk but comfortable pace, letting the day's watchfulness settle. (out_of_house); 18:30 Return home, wash my hands, reheat my dinner, and eat at the dining table. I check on Julian briefly and bring him anything practical he requests. (kitchen); 19:30 Read or watch a quiet program with Julian in the living room, keeping the volume low and checking discreetly whether he needs water, painkillers, or another blanket. When I finish reading, I leave the book face-down with its receipt marking the page on the small shelving unit by the sofa. (living_room); 21:30 Wash the dishes, check Julian's temperature, put painkillers and water within reach, and make sure he is settled for the night. (kitchen); 22:00 Wash my face, brush my teeth, change into sleep clothes, and put my reading glasses on the nightstand by my bed. (bedroom_1); 22:25 Put my phone on charge by the wardrobe, read a few pages in bed, then turn out the light and go to sleep. (bedroom_1)
> *mara thinks:* Julian is still ill on day three, so I will keep my usual Friday work structure but check on him quietly at breakfast, during work breaks, and before bed. I will handle simple food rather than expect him to cook. Friday invoices may raise stress slightly, but the day should remain manageable.

**julian plans the day** (stress 1, tired 3, mood 1, health 1): 07:30 Wake slowly, take my temperature, drink water, and sit on the edge of the bed before getting up. (bedroom_2); 08:00 Wash, brush my teeth, and change into comfortable clothes, leaving my sleep clothes in the hamper. (bathroom_2); 08:30 Make weak tea and carry a small breakfast of crackers and a little banana to the living-room sofa, then eat slowly. (kitchen); 09:15 Rest or sleep on the large sofa with my phone nearby, keeping museum notifications silenced except for anything genuinely urgent. (living_room); 11:30 Check my temperature, take an ordinary dose of medicine after checking the label, and refill my water. (living_room); 11:45 Send one brief museum message confirming that I remain ill and will be unavailable today, then put the phone back on silent. (bedroom_2); 12:30 Eat a small lunch at the kitchen island if I can manage it, using whatever light food is available, and leave the dishes by the sink afterward. (kitchen); 13:15 Return to the large sofa for quiet rest, dozing or listening to low music and asking Mara for help only if I genuinely need it. (living_room); 15:30 Take a lukewarm shower, brush my teeth, and put on fresh comfortable clothes. (bathroom_2); 16:00 Rest in the second bedroom with the phone on silent, making only a short note of any museum matter that can wait. (bedroom_2); 17:30 Order a simple dinner and drinks by phone rather than cooking, then clear a small space at the dining table. (living_room); 18:00 Collect the delivered food, carry it to the dining table, and eat a small portion slowly. (living_room); 18:30 Put the remaining food into a container and leave the used plate and fork by the sink, then return to the sofa. (kitchen); 19:30 Watch something quiet or listen to low music from the large sofa, keeping conversation minimal while sharing the room if Mara is there. (living_room); 21:00 Check my temperature once more, wash my face, brush my teeth, and refill the water bottle. (bathroom_2); 21:30 Get into bed, silence work notifications completely, and leave the phone and water within reach on the nightstand. (bedroom_2); 22:00 Rest quietly and try to sleep, using tissues or water if needed. (bedroom_2)

**mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 08:30 Wake, wash my face, brush my teeth, dress in comfortable clothes, and make sure the phone is not still beside the bed. I carry my reading glasses from the nightstand if I need them. (bedroom_1); 09:00 Make coffee and a quiet breakfast, carrying the mug and food to the dining table. I put the tea bag and small brass spoon on the kitchen sink-cabinet countertop for later. (kitchen); 09:30 Change the bedding, carrying the used bed linen to the hamper and bringing the clean bed linen into place. I sort the laundry into workable loads and start one load if the machines are available. (bedroom_1); 12:00 Water the balcony plants, carrying the watering can out and back, then wash my hands and have a glass of water. (balcony); 12:30 Make a simple lunch, carry it to the dining table, and clear the dishes afterward. I leave the kitchen ready for the evening meal rather than beginning any elaborate cooking. (kitchen); 13:15 Read for a while in the living room, carrying the book and reading glasses from the bedroom. I leave the book face-down with its receipt marking the page when I stop. (living_room); 14:00 Do a contained editing block at home, opening the laptop and taking the active manuscript from the canvas folder. I review a few pages only, save the file, and return the proof pages to the folder. (bedroom_1); 16:00 Close the laptop, make tea, and take it to the living room. I tidy the current papers into the canvas folder and leave the tea bags and brass spoon on the kitchen counter rather than putting them away yet. (kitchen); 16:30 Shower and dress for the evening meal, choosing comfortable but presentable clothes. I put away the laundry that is ready and leave the remaining sorting for tomorrow if necessary. (bathroom_1); 17:30 Set the dining table and prepare a small salad or other simple contribution to the meal, carrying plates, glasses, and cutlery from the kitchen. I keep the preparation light so Julian can manage his own cooking plans. (kitchen); 18:30 Share the evening meal with Julian and our friend. I listen closely, add precise observations when they occur to me, and help carry dishes or water as needed. When the unexpected outage interrupts the evening, I stop trying to monitor the schedule, use the available candle or flashlight, and sit with them through the unstructured pause. (living_room); 21:30 See our friend out, collect the most vulnerable dishes from the living room, and leave the rest safely grouped in the kitchen rather than attempting a full cleanup if the outage is still affecting things. (kitchen); 22:00 Wash my face and brush my teeth, change into sleep clothes, and put my reading glasses on the nightstand by the bed. (bathroom_1); 22:30 Put my phone on charge by the wardrobe, read a few pages in bed if the power has returned or use the flashlight briefly, then turn out the light and sleep. (bedroom_1)

**julian plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 07:30 Wake slowly, drink water, check my temperature, and sit on the edge of the bed while I work out the new commute timing. (bedroom_2); 07:45 Wash, brush my teeth, get dressed in work clothes, and put my sleep clothes in the hamper. (bathroom_2); 08:20 Make a quick breakfast and coffee, eat in the kitchen, and rinse the mug and bowl at the sink. (kitchen); 08:35 Pack my laptop and work materials, fill my travel mug and water bottle, and check that my badge and keys are with me. (bedroom_2); 08:50 Put on my jacket, collect my badge and keys from the hallway tray, and leave for the museum using the revised commute. (hallway); 09:00 Work at the museum under the new Sunday schedule, noting the changed start and commute pattern and handling the day’s exhibition tasks. (out_of_house); 12:30 Return home, put my badge and keys in the hallway tray, and make a straightforward lunch rather than attempting an elaborate Sunday cooking session. (kitchen); 13:15 Eat lunch at the kitchen island and pack the remaining food into a leftover container for another meal. (kitchen); 13:35 Wash the plate and cooking things, clear the oil and wooden spoon from the island, and leave the kitchen in a usable state. (kitchen); 14:00 Rest on the large sofa with my phone nearby, keeping museum notifications quiet while I let the new commute settle. (living_room); 15:00 Call my mother from the sofa and tell her about the museum schedule change, treating it as a puzzle rather than complaining about it. (living_room); 15:40 Read the weekend newspapers on the large sofa, making a few notes about the commute timing and leaving the papers there for now. (living_room); 16:45 Review the week ahead and write a short practical checklist for museum transitions, including what to pack and when to leave. (living_room); 17:15 Take a shower, change into comfortable evening clothes, and put my work clothes in the hamper. (bathroom_2); 18:00 Make a light evening meal from the lunch leftovers, heat it safely, and leave the cooking oil and wooden spoon on the island while I work. (kitchen); 18:30 Eat at the dining table, drink water, and put the used plate and utensils by the sink afterward. (living_room); 19:00 Put the leftover container in the fridge, wash the saucepan and plate, and finally clear the oil and wooden spoon from the island. (kitchen); 19:20 Read quietly in the living room, with the phone on silent, and leave the weekend newspapers on the sofa rather than reorganising the room. (living_room); 21:00 Prepare for bed, brush my teeth, refill my water bottle, and put the phone on the nightstand. (bathroom_2); 21:30 Get into bed and review tomorrow’s departure plan briefly, then put on my sleep mask and rest. (bedroom_2)
> *julian thinks:* I wake recovered from yesterday but still take the morning gently. The museum schedule change starts today, so I treat the altered commute as a practical puzzle: I check the new timing, leave earlier than usual, and keep the transition home deliberately simple. **mara plans the day** (stress 1, tired 1, mood 2, health 3): 08:00 Wake, wash my face, brush my teeth, and get dressed in comfortable daytime clothes. I collect my phone from the nightstand by the wardrobe and leave the reading glasses on the nightstand by my bed. (bedroom_1); 08:30 Make coffee and carry the mug to the dining table. I eat a small breakfast and leave the mug there while I look over the kitchen after yesterday's outage. (kitchen); 09:00 Collect the grouped dishes and glasses from the living room and carry them to the kitchen. I wash the most vulnerable pieces, wipe the dining table, and leave the remaining kitchen things arranged for later rather than doing a deep clean. (kitchen); 09:40 Sort the remaining laundry and put away anything dry. I carry the laundry hamper between the bedroom and the laundry area, leaving an unfinished load for another time if needed. (bedroom_1); 10:30 Read with breakfast and plan the coming editing week at the dining table. I carry my book and blue reading-glasses case from the bedroom, write a short list in my notebook, and return the book face-down with its receipt marking the page to the small shelving unit by the sofa when I finish. (living_room); 12:00 Stay out of Julian's way while he prepares his usual large lunch. I set out a plate and drinking glass for myself when the meal is ready, carry them to the dining table, and eat without assuming he will want a long conversation. (living_room); 13:00 Carry my plate and glass to the kitchen, wash them, and put any food Julian has designated as leftovers into the leftover container. I leave the kitchen ready for the afternoon. (kitchen); 13:30 Do a contained editing block in the bedroom. I take the canvas folder from the desk, remove the current proof pages, open the laptop, review and mark a limited section, then save the work and return the proof pages to the folder. (bedroom_1); 15:15 Close the laptop and take a quiet break with water. I carry my reading glasses to the living room in their blue case and sit briefly, observing whether Julian seems available for conversation rather than pressing him. (living_room); 16:00 Prepare simple meals and label the week's leftovers. I carry ingredients and containers to the kitchen, make a salad and portion what needs saving, then wipe the counter. I put the tea bag and small brass spoon on the sink-cabinet countertop for later and leave the spoon there. (kitchen); 17:00 Make tea and carry it to the living room. I read a little or talk with Julian if the timing feels natural, leaving the mug on the end table when my hands are occupied. (living_room); 18:00 Make a light dinner from the prepared food, carry plates and water to the dining table, and eat with Julian. I keep conversation flexible because his energy may be brisk or unexpectedly lively. (kitchen); 19:00 Carry the dishes to the kitchen, wash them, and leave the labeled leftovers together in the refrigerator. I do only the necessary cleanup. (kitchen); 19:30 Shower, change into evening clothes, and settle the bedroom for sleep. I use moisturizer and hand cream, then leave the reading glasses on the top of the nightstand by the bed. (bathroom_1); 21:00 Read a few pages in bed, carrying the book from the living room and returning it to the small shelving unit by the sofa afterward. I put the phone on charge by the wardrobe, out of arm's reach. (bedroom_1); 22:00 Turn out the light, put on the sleep mask, and sleep. (bedroom_1)
